## 2026-11-26T03:00:00Z — Higher-arity projected-rank replay on B677 (owner item 4)

### What was done (commands, files, features)

- Regenerated the #5097 eleven-query stream with #5184's `exact` arm. Its rows hash to
  `f1bbab69…`, byte-identical to #5097. `research/higher_arity_rank_replay_20261126/tools/replay.py`
  runs an independent Python exact replay under the fb2 rule (at least two factor-base terms)
  and the all-arity rule.
- At 645 rows: fb2 gives LP rank 161 and projected rank 452, matching #5076; all arities give
  LP rank 164 and point projected rank 481 with the target not identifiable, matching #5122.
  At 707 rows the target is identifiable at row 706 (fb2) or row 670 (all); the log is
  987654321 under both rules.
- Added `ADMIT=fb2|all` to `examples/pair_table_rank_rate.rs` (default fb2). Ran paired
  panels: 40 salts, then 400. With all arities: queries and probes −5.0% [−5.1, −4.9], lower
  in 400/400 salts; probe time −4.4%; elimination +5.3%; cold time −1.4% [−3.1, +0.2], not
  significant.
- Fixed a duplicate `pub mod semaev_selector;` in `src/cryptanalysis/mod.rs` (E0428). It came
  from the main merge into the #5188 branch; the fix was pushed to that branch as `2f5a5026`.

### Decisions made (and why)

- "Higher arity" means relations with three or four large-prime terms, which the #5097
  consumer excludes. That is the definition in #5076/#5122.
- Collection work is measured on the pair table, because #5097's full-table source cannot
  stop before a query is finished.
- Stacked on #5188, since #5202 was merged into that branch.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)

- A cheaper consumer path for higher-arity rows (they carry up to four LP columns) would
  turn the 5% collection saving into wall time.
- Owner item 5: Lean depth certificates.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)

- The #5202 branch had been deleted after its merge, so I worked from the #5188 branch.
- The #5188 branch did not compile after the main merge (duplicate module); fixed as above.

## 2026-11-26 ~04:50Z — Literature and existing-code review completed (Codex)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- Reviewed `src/isogeny/{mod,velu,volcano,graph}.rs`, `research/isogeny_vs_mu_factor_base_20261102/README.md`, its `lean/VeluObstruction.lean`, and `isogeny_algos/src/find/sea.rs`.
- Read the relevant primary sources: Vélu, *Isogenies between elliptic curves* (1971 translation, https://aghitza.github.io/publications/translation-velu.pdf); Sutherland, *Isogeny volcanoes* (ANTS X, https://msp.org/obs/2013/1-1/obs-v1-n1-p25-s.pdf); Couveignes--Dewaghe--Morain, *Isogeny cycles and the Schoof-Elkies-Atkin algorithm* (https://www.lix.polytechnique.fr/Labo/Francois.Morain/Articles/isog-cycles.pdf).
- Tried the required KB fallback with `scripts/research-kb status`; no `research-kb` executable or catalog is available in this environment. GitHub repository search was used to locate the existing code and studies instead.
- Ran the required Conductor reservation command for `examples/rational_2_isogeny_floor_crater.rs`; it could not run because `conductor` is not installed.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Reuse `SmallCurve`, `volcano::neighbors_ell`, `velu_isogeny_2`, `j_invariant`, and `cm_discriminant` in one native Rust example instead of copying field or Vélu arithmetic.
- Use the short-Weierstrass form `y^2 = x^3 + 9x + 15` over `F_17`, isomorphic to `y^2 = x(x-1)(x-3)`, and verify only explicit facts: three rational kernels, one base-field isomorphic quotient, two quotients with only the dual rational kernel, and no five-step non-backtracking chain. Volcano labels will be presented as the standard interpretation of those checked facts, not inferred from matching j-invariants alone.
- Treat immediate dual-edge repetition separately: it gives arbitrarily long rational chains but not cyclic composites. This follows the repository's existing `VeluObstruction.lean` and SEA terminology.

### Decisions made (and why)

  advantage; moreover `97 > K/2=84` and exact target uniqueness is not observed.

  uniqueness as distinct events. This prevents a balanced circuit from being
  reported as a recovered target.

## 2026-11-26 ~04:50Z — Literature and existing-code review completed (Codex)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- Reviewed `src/isogeny/{mod,velu,volcano,graph}.rs`, `research/isogeny_vs_mu_factor_base_20261102/README.md`, its `lean/VeluObstruction.lean`, and `isogeny_algos/src/find/sea.rs`.
- Read the relevant primary sources: Vélu, *Isogenies between elliptic curves* (1971 translation, https://aghitza.github.io/publications/translation-velu.pdf); Sutherland, *Isogeny volcanoes* (ANTS X, https://msp.org/obs/2013/1-1/obs-v1-n1-p25-s.pdf); Couveignes--Dewaghe--Morain, *Isogeny cycles and the Schoof-Elkies-Atkin algorithm* (https://www.lix.polytechnique.fr/Labo/Francois.Morain/Articles/isog-cycles.pdf).
- Tried the required KB fallback with `scripts/research-kb status`; no `research-kb` executable or catalog is available in this environment. GitHub repository search was used to locate the existing code and studies instead.
- Ran the required Conductor reservation command for `examples/rational_2_isogeny_floor_crater.rs`; it could not run because `conductor` is not installed.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Reuse `SmallCurve`, `volcano::neighbors_ell`, `velu_isogeny_2`, `j_invariant`, and `cm_discriminant` in one native Rust example instead of copying field or Vélu arithmetic.
- Use the short-Weierstrass form `y^2 = x^3 + 9x + 15` over `F_17`, isomorphic to `y^2 = x(x-1)(x-3)`, and verify only explicit facts: three rational kernels, one base-field isomorphic quotient, two quotients with only the dual rational kernel, and no five-step non-backtracking chain. Volcano labels will be presented as the standard interpretation of those checked facts, not inferred from matching j-invariants alone.
- Treat immediate dual-edge repetition separately: it gives arbitrarily long rational chains but not cyclic composites. This follows the repository's existing `VeluObstruction.lean` and SEA terminology.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- Implement the runnable example with embedded tests, then run focused and required repository checks.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)
- KB and Conductor services are unavailable locally; both failures are recorded rather than silently skipped. No path conflict could be queried, so edits remain confined to the newly created example plus task/log metadata.

- Read the relevant primary sources: Vélu, *Isogenies between elliptic curves* (1971 translation, https://aghitza.github.io/publications/translation-velu.pdf); Sutherland, *Isogeny volcanoes* (ANTS X, https://msp.org/obs/2013/1-1/obs-v1-n1-p25-s.pdf); Couveignes--Dewaghe--Morain, *Isogeny cycles and the Schoof-Elkies-Atkin algorithm* (https://www.lix.polytechnique.fr/Labo/Francois.Morain/Articles/isog-cycles.pdf).
- Tried the required KB fallback with `scripts/research-kb status`; no `research-kb` executable or catalog is available in this environment. GitHub repository search was used to locate the existing code and studies instead.
- Ran the required Conductor reservation command for `examples/rational_2_isogeny_floor_crater.rs`; it could not run because `conductor` is not installed.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Reuse `SmallCurve`, `volcano::neighbors_ell`, `velu_isogeny_2`, `j_invariant`, and `cm_discriminant` in one native Rust example instead of copying field or Vélu arithmetic.
- Use the short-Weierstrass form `y^2 = x^3 + 9x + 15` over `F_17`, isomorphic to `y^2 = x(x-1)(x-3)`, and verify only explicit facts: three rational kernels, one base-field isomorphic quotient, two quotients with only the dual rational kernel, and no five-step non-backtracking chain. Volcano labels will be presented as the standard interpretation of those checked facts, not inferred from matching j-invariants alone.
- Treat immediate dual-edge repetition separately: it gives arbitrarily long rational chains but not cyclic composites. This follows the repository's existing `VeluObstruction.lean` and SEA terminology.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- Implement the runnable example with embedded tests, then run focused and required repository checks.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)
- KB and Conductor services are unavailable locally; both failures are recorded rather than silently skipped. No path conflict could be queried, so edits remain confined to the newly created example plus task/log metadata.

## 2026-11-26T07:43Z — PKM setup source review completed (Codex, draft PR #5236)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- Read the primary PKM paper: Petit, Kosters, and Messeng, *Algebraic
  Approaches for the Elliptic Curve Discrete Logarithm Problem over Prime
  Fields*, PKC 2016, especially §3.4 (IACR PDF
  `https://www.iacr.org/archive/pkc2016/96140156/96140156.pdf`). It requires
  an auxiliary curve over the same field with a suitable smooth subgroup and
  decomposes its kernel isogeny into low-degree Vélu maps; it does not impose
  ordinarity on the auxiliary curve.

  references resolved and no overfull boxes. New-paper prose lint reports zero
  findings; all mathematical assertions remain `[pcc:unverified]`.
- Added successor Theorem 18, an exact direct-sum bound for static-provenance
  product trees with arbitrary target-adaptive query branching. For `D`
  pre-target candidate leaves and `B` uniform targets it proves
  `E[Z] <= BD/n`; charging the explicit provenance and optimizing under
  `D <= N^m` gives the fixed-arity exponent `m/(2m-1)`. The proof does not use
  a random-map heuristic, but it deliberately excludes compact coordinate
  circuits that generate new target-dependent subtrees. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Extended the finite Rust checker with the multiplicity identity underlying
  Theorem 18 and updated the proof map, decision gates, and explicit boundary.
- Added Proposition 19, identifying balanced split-tree witness recovery with
  group `k`SUM-Indexing. Audited Dinur--Golovnev's ICALP 2026 trade-off in an
  attacker-favourable model: after substituting exact relation-output demand,
  its exponent is
  `1/2 + (3/4)/(2m - 1/2 - 2 delta) > 1/2` for `m >= 3`. This assumes, without
  claiming, free transport from integer to elliptic-group inputs and ignores
  extra preprocessing work, so restoring omitted costs cannot improve the
  exponent. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Recorded the primary data-structure sources and the lower-bound frontier in
  `LITERATURE.md`. The 2023/2026 papers confirm that strong adaptive
  3SUM-Indexing bounds beyond the logarithmic/tiny-probe regime remain a major
  open data-structure problem; this calibrates rather than proves the full
  ARQP conjecture. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 20, the exact exponent balance for an implicit one-witness
  data structure with preprocessing `N^s` and query cost `N^t`. The
  factor-base cap creates two regimes: if `s-t >= 1`, the optimized exponent
  is `s/(s+m-1-t)` and crosses rho exactly when `s+t < m-1`; if `s-t < 1`,
  the optimum approaches `alpha=1/m` and crosses rho exactly when
  `t < m/2-1`. Replacing `s` by `max(s,2)` incorporates the conventional
  `N^2` sparse linear-algebra charge. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Replayed `examples/finite_count_check.rs` in the isolated
  `rust:1.83-alpine` image pinned by digest
  `sha256:0ac946ed7597a9f053a1be2ce38c09aa88b3d7079a91ea491493615294b1f699`.
  All three tests passed, including direct grid minimization of Theorem 20's
  piecewise formula. This resolves the earlier missing-host-toolchain issue
  without claiming independent verification. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 20 by removing its one-witness restriction. The exact
  tuple-count bound applies to every verified decomposition returned across a
  batch, so `t` now denotes amortized target-dependent work after arbitrary
  multipoint sharing. Output and verification remain charged; the same
  piecewise exponent formula follows. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added exact `m=3,4,5` sanity rows for the audited 2026 indexing family over
  `delta in {0,1/2,1}`. The resulting fractions range from `11/19` to `5/7`;
  all remain strictly above one-half. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Extended and replayed the native checker for those nine exact fractions.
  The pinned Rust container now reports four passing tests, zero failures.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 21 for exact homomorphic bucket filters. A homomorphism out of
  the prime-order subgroup is trivial or injective; global algebraic-group
  maps from an elliptic curve to `G_a` or `G_m` are trivial; and an isogeny of
  degree below the subgroup order is injective on that subgroup. This closes
  exact additive/multiplicative quotient labels and low-degree isogeny fibres,
  but not non-homomorphic or one-sided-error filters. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Replayed the expanded native checker in the pinned Rust container: five
  tests passed and zero failed, including the small prime cyclic-group
  homomorphism kernel. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 21 from group-valued homomorphisms to arbitrary exact
  compositional labels `psi(P+Q)=psi(P) star psi(Q)`. Equality of labels is a
  group congruence whose fibres are cosets of a subgroup, even when `star` has
  no assumed algebraic laws; prime order again forces constant or injective
  labels. The remaining filters must therefore be non-compositional,
  approximate, or one-sided-error. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added an exhaustive finite replay over every smaller label map for cyclic
  prime orders 3, 5, and 7, accepting an arbitrary derived binary label table.
  The pinned Rust replay reports six passing tests and zero failures.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 21 again: any exact predicate of separate labels
  `R(lambda_A(P),lambda_B(Q),lambda_T(T))` for `P+Q=T` forces all three label
  maps to be injective, without assuming compositionality. The unresolved
  bucket filters must allow false positives, use target-specific functions,
  or avoid factoring through separate labels. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Exhaustively replayed the separate-label predicate obstruction for small
  cyclic groups. The pinned Rust run now has seven passing tests and zero
  failures. A `rustfmt` component check could not run because the pinned
  container's component download repeatedly failed; compilation itself is
  warning-free under the standalone test command. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 22 for one-sided separate-label filters. Collisions in either
  partial-sum label map inject into distinct false accepted triples, giving an
  exact label-fibre second-moment lower bound and, for uniform target batches,
  an expected false-verification lower bound. Joint encodings,
  target-dependent labels, and non-enumerating recovery remain outside the
  theorem. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded cyclic-group replay of the one-sided collision bound. The
  pinned Rust container reports eight passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 22 to target-label collisions. Choosing one witness per
  attainable target injects ordered collisions inside each target-label fibre
  into distinct false triples, giving the same second-moment obstruction for
  all three separate label maps. The native fixture now replays all three
  bounds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 23 as a constructive target-dependent boundary. The label
  `x(T-P)` against a hash table keyed by `x(Q)` has only a sign false positive,
  but must scan a partial-sum side. Its best separate-query split has exponent
  `(m-1)/(2m-3)`, giving `2/3`, `3/5`, and `4/7` for arities 3, 4, and 5.
  This isolates search, rather than false verification, as the remaining
  bottleneck. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added an exact native arithmetic replay for the target-shift exponents and
  their comparison with explicit batching. The pinned Rust container reports
  nine passing tests and zero failures in 0.19 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 24, an unconditional preprocessing/query direct sum in the
  degree-explicit algebraic-selector model. On each normalized correspondence
  curve, a degree-`d` leaf morphism hits the `N`-point factor base at no more
  than `dN` witness points; summing over charged branch projection-degree mass
  `Delta` gives `E[Z] <= B N Delta / n`. Collecting
  `Theta(N)` rows forces `B Delta = Omega(n)` and hence `Omega(sqrt(n))` total
  work. Adaptive branch choice, multivalued root recovery, and direct
  non-materializing leaf output are included; compact shared DAGs without
  expanded-degree charging remain open.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded finite replay of the selector fibre-mass counting kernel.
  The pinned Rust container reports ten passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 25 to prevent an invalid extension of the degree-explicit
  model: multiplication-by-`k` has degree `k^2` but an `O(log k)`
  double-and-add DAG. Expanded degree is therefore not compact-circuit work.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 26, a degree-free direct sum for compact single-valued global
  maps. The rigidity lemma makes every `E -> E` map a translation of an
  endomorphism, whose restriction to the prime-order subgroup is constant or
  injective. Each explicit branch therefore covers at most `N` targets,
  giving `E[Z] <= B N r / n` and `Omega(sqrt(n))` total work for an
  `r`-branch catalogue. Succinct correspondences and implicitly exponential
  branching remain outside the theorem. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a finite cyclic replay of the constant-or-injective branch bound. The
  pinned Rust container reports eleven passing tests and zero failures in
  0.20 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 27 for the finite-domain boundary. The reduced coordinate
  algebra on the subgroup points is the full function algebra, so arbitrary
  relation selectors have dense `O(n)` lookup representations and their leaf
  functions need not be constant or injective. This table is not sub-rho, but
  it proves that global morphism rigidity cannot settle succinct finite-domain
  circuits; a genuine compression/query lower bound is required.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded cyclic example whose factor-base triples cover every target
  and whose selected leaf functions are neither constant nor injective. The
  pinned Rust container reports twelve passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 28 for symbolic recovery from succinct correspondences. A
  straight-line query that remains in the target function field and recovers
  a generic root defines a rational section; composing with its leaf
  projections yields the global rational maps already covered by Theorem 26.
  A remaining escape must use algebraic root extraction, finite-field
  branching, or finite-domain-only correctness. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 29 as an exact finite-field root-recovery counterexample.
  For primes `p = 3 mod 4`, the generic correspondence `y^2=t` has no section
  over `F_p(t)`, while `t^((p+1)/4)` selects a square root on every quadratic
  residue using `O(log p)` multiplications. This is not an elliptic relation
  algorithm, but it blocks any degree- or size-only extension of Theorem 28.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded native replay of the square-root selector. The pinned
  Rust container reports thirteen passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 30, showing that even per-leaf factor-base hit bounds fail
  for arbitrary finite-domain circuits. If `d` divides `p-1`, the compact map
  `t^((p-1)/d)` sends every target into the `d+1` element set consisting of
  zero and the `d`-th roots of unity; fixed branches can relabel that set by
  elliptic factor-base points. The map does not solve the joint decomposition
  constraint, which is therefore essential to any remaining barrier.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a native replay of the exact power-map image size. The pinned
  Rust container reports fourteen passing tests and zero failures in
  0.20 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 31 for the exact joint-entropy boundary. Any valid
  selector from targets to `m` factor-base leaves is injective because the sum
  of its output tuple recovers the target. Hence `|U| <= N^m`, with joint
  entropy `log |U|`; at least one leaf image has size `|U|^(1/m)`. This is
  tight for a set-theoretic choice of one tuple per nonempty sum fibre, so it
  reproduces tuple counting without a computational lower bound.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Extended the bounded finite-domain selector replay with the joint-image and
  product-of-leaf-images inequalities. The pinned Rust container still
  reports fourteen passing tests and zero failures, now in 0.19 seconds.
  `[pcc:unverified]`

### Decisions made (and why)

- Keep every new mathematical assertion `[pcc:unverified]` until its proof map,

### What was done
- PR #5081 merged on 2026-11-25. Restarted branch `claude/hyperelliptic-index-calculus-td1fdn` from main (faeb7c8937f). Added the ACTIVE item (106f608fbbd) and opened draft PR #5216.
- **New switch.** `QLinAlg::SgeCoord` gains `fallback: bool`. The new arm `2lp-sfb-sge-nofb` (record label `Q_diem_2lp_sfb_sge_nofb`) skips the full-core Wiedemann fallback.
- **New counters.** `la_fallbacks_skipped` counts the fallbacks the new arm skipped. `la_fallback_rescues` counts the baseline attempts its fallback certified.
- **New test.** `sfb_sge_nofb_pairs_with_sge` checks: without a rescue, both arms have the same attempts, relations and `rel`; linear algebra is equal with no fallback and strictly lower in the new arm with one.
- **Drafted** `research/quartic_diem_nofb_20261126/`: PROTOCOL.md, `seeds.json` (seed policy v1), `analyze.py`, `run_main.sh`, `run_131101.sh`.

- Removed the stale `target/` (2.3 GB) to free disk: 4.7 GB free.

### Decisions made
- **Why the pairing is exact.** The fallback already draws from `rng.clone()`, and `skip_wiedemann_draws` advances the main stream in both arms. So skipping it leaves every later draw unchanged.
- **Decision rule.** IMPROVEMENT requires the order to hold across the band, with the w = 0 ratio ≤ 1 and no tolerance (lesson L-50756e1568b4).
- **Execution route.** Local, not EKS: this container has no AWS identity. Recorded in PROTOCOL §7.

- Build the binary, freeze, run 160 runs, write RESULTS, figure and PDF, run the validator, add the benchmarks row.

### Decisions made (and why)

- Use the short-Weierstrass form `y^2 = x^3 + 9x + 15` over `F_17`, isomorphic to `y^2 = x(x-1)(x-3)`, and verify only explicit facts: three rational kernels, one base-field isomorphic quotient, two quotients with only the dual rational kernel, and no five-step non-backtracking chain. Volcano labels will be presented as the standard interpretation of those checked facts, not inferred from matching j-invariants alone.
- Treat immediate dual-edge repetition separately: it gives arbitrarily long rational chains but not cyclic composites. This follows the repository's existing `VeluObstruction.lean` and SEA terminology.

- Tried the required KB fallback with `scripts/research-kb status`; no `research-kb` executable or catalog is available in this environment. GitHub repository search was used to locate the existing code and studies instead.
- Ran the required Conductor reservation command for `examples/rational_2_isogeny_floor_crater.rs`; it could not run because `conductor` is not installed.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Reuse `SmallCurve`, `volcano::neighbors_ell`, `velu_isogeny_2`, `j_invariant`, and `cm_discriminant` in one native Rust example instead of copying field or Vélu arithmetic.
- Use the short-Weierstrass form `y^2 = x^3 + 9x + 15` over `F_17`, isomorphic to `y^2 = x(x-1)(x-3)`, and verify only explicit facts: three rational kernels, one base-field isomorphic quotient, two quotients with only the dual rational kernel, and no five-step non-backtracking chain. Volcano labels will be presented as the standard interpretation of those checked facts, not inferred from matching j-invariants alone.
- Treat immediate dual-edge repetition separately: it gives arbitrarily long rational chains but not cyclic composites. This follows the repository's existing `VeluObstruction.lean` and SEA terminology.

### Open threads (unfinished, pick up next time)
- Implement the runnable example with embedded tests, then run focused and required repository checks.

### Errors (what went wrong, how it was handled)
- KB and Conductor services are unavailable locally; both failures are recorded rather than silently skipped. No path conflict could be queried, so edits remain confined to the newly created example plus task/log metadata.

### Decisions made (and why)
- Classify the implementation as a generic-decomposition component control.
  Amadori builds a dynamic random factor base and asks for one `m`-term
  dependency; PKM uses a composed algebraic selector and polynomial solving.
  A two-plus-three meet-in-the-middle table does not become either algebraic
  method merely because its points come from the Dickson selector.
- Use the repository's actual `p = 2^31 - 1`, `t = 6` Dickson/T2 factor-base
  builder and `m = 5`. Keep both point signs, return five factor-base indices,
  and verify them with the existing affine group implementation.
- Optimize affine conversion with Montgomery batch inversion. This changes a
  constant and not the `O(B^3)` setup / `O(B^2)` query exponents, so no
  asymptotic or algebraic-extractor claim will be made.

## 2026-11-26T07:43Z — PKM setup source review completed (Codex, draft PR #5236)

### What was done (commands, files, features)
- Read the primary PKM paper: Petit, Kosters, and Messeng, *Algebraic
  Approaches for the Elliptic Curve Discrete Logarithm Problem over Prime
  Fields*, PKC 2016, especially §3.4 (IACR PDF
  `https://www.iacr.org/archive/pkc2016/96140156/96140156.pdf`). It requires
  an auxiliary curve over the same field with a suitable smooth subgroup and
  decomposes its kernel isogeny into low-degree Vélu maps; it does not impose

  product trees with arbitrary target-adaptive query branching. For `D`
  pre-target candidate leaves and `B` uniform targets it proves
  `E[Z] <= BD/n`; charging the explicit provenance and optimizing under
  `D <= N^m` gives the fixed-arity exponent `m/(2m-1)`. The proof does not use
  a random-map heuristic, but it deliberately excludes compact coordinate
  circuits that generate new target-dependent subtrees. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Extended the finite Rust checker with the multiplicity identity underlying
  Theorem 18 and updated the proof map, decision gates, and explicit boundary.
- Added Proposition 19, identifying balanced split-tree witness recovery with
  group `k`SUM-Indexing. Audited Dinur--Golovnev's ICALP 2026 trade-off in an
  attacker-favourable model: after substituting exact relation-output demand,
  its exponent is
  `1/2 + (3/4)/(2m - 1/2 - 2 delta) > 1/2` for `m >= 3`. This assumes, without
  claiming, free transport from integer to elliptic-group inputs and ignores
  extra preprocessing work, so restoring omitted costs cannot improve the
  exponent. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Recorded the primary data-structure sources and the lower-bound frontier in
  `LITERATURE.md`. The 2023/2026 papers confirm that strong adaptive
  3SUM-Indexing bounds beyond the logarithmic/tiny-probe regime remain a major
  open data-structure problem; this calibrates rather than proves the full
  ARQP conjecture. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 20, the exact exponent balance for an implicit one-witness
  data structure with preprocessing `N^s` and query cost `N^t`. The
  factor-base cap creates two regimes: if `s-t >= 1`, the optimized exponent
  is `s/(s+m-1-t)` and crosses rho exactly when `s+t < m-1`; if `s-t < 1`,
  the optimum approaches `alpha=1/m` and crosses rho exactly when
  `t < m/2-1`. Replacing `s` by `max(s,2)` incorporates the conventional
  `N^2` sparse linear-algebra charge. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Replayed `examples/finite_count_check.rs` in the isolated
  `rust:1.83-alpine` image pinned by digest
  `sha256:0ac946ed7597a9f053a1be2ce38c09aa88b3d7079a91ea491493615294b1f699`.
  All three tests passed, including direct grid minimization of Theorem 20's
  piecewise formula. This resolves the earlier missing-host-toolchain issue
  without claiming independent verification. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 20 by removing its one-witness restriction. The exact
  tuple-count bound applies to every verified decomposition returned across a
  batch, so `t` now denotes amortized target-dependent work after arbitrary
  multipoint sharing. Output and verification remain charged; the same
  piecewise exponent formula follows. `[pcc:unverified]`

  `delta in {0,1/2,1}`. The resulting fractions range from `11/19` to `5/7`;

  The pinned Rust container now reports four passing tests, zero failures.

- Added Theorem 21 for exact homomorphic bucket filters. A homomorphism out of
  the prime-order subgroup is trivial or injective; global algebraic-group
  maps from an elliptic curve to `G_a` or `G_m` are trivial; and an isogeny of
  degree below the subgroup order is injective on that subgroup. This closes
  exact additive/multiplicative quotient labels and low-degree isogeny fibres,
  but not non-homomorphic or one-sided-error filters. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Replayed the expanded native checker in the pinned Rust container: five
  tests passed and zero failed, including the small prime cyclic-group
  homomorphism kernel. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 21 from group-valued homomorphisms to arbitrary exact
  compositional labels `psi(P+Q)=psi(P) star psi(Q)`. Equality of labels is a
  group congruence whose fibres are cosets of a subgroup, even when `star` has
  no assumed algebraic laws; prime order again forces constant or injective
  labels. The remaining filters must therefore be non-compositional,
  approximate, or one-sided-error. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added an exhaustive finite replay over every smaller label map for cyclic
  prime orders 3, 5, and 7, accepting an arbitrary derived binary label table.
  The pinned Rust replay reports six passing tests and zero failures.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 21 again: any exact predicate of separate labels
  `R(lambda_A(P),lambda_B(Q),lambda_T(T))` for `P+Q=T` forces all three label
  maps to be injective, without assuming compositionality. The unresolved
  bucket filters must allow false positives, use target-specific functions,
  or avoid factoring through separate labels. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Exhaustively replayed the separate-label predicate obstruction for small
  cyclic groups. The pinned Rust run now has seven passing tests and zero
  failures. A `rustfmt` component check could not run because the pinned
  container's component download repeatedly failed; compilation itself is
  warning-free under the standalone test command. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 22 for one-sided separate-label filters. Collisions in either
  partial-sum label map inject into distinct false accepted triples, giving an
  exact label-fibre second-moment lower bound and, for uniform target batches,
  an expected false-verification lower bound. Joint encodings,
  target-dependent labels, and non-enumerating recovery remain outside the
  theorem. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded cyclic-group replay of the one-sided collision bound. The
  pinned Rust container reports eight passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 22 to target-label collisions. Choosing one witness per
  attainable target injects ordered collisions inside each target-label fibre
  into distinct false triples, giving the same second-moment obstruction for
  all three separate label maps. The native fixture now replays all three
  bounds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 23 as a constructive target-dependent boundary. The label
  `x(T-P)` against a hash table keyed by `x(Q)` has only a sign false positive,
  but must scan a partial-sum side. Its best separate-query split has exponent
  `(m-1)/(2m-3)`, giving `2/3`, `3/5`, and `4/7` for arities 3, 4, and 5.
  This isolates search, rather than false verification, as the remaining
  bottleneck. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added an exact native arithmetic replay for the target-shift exponents and
  their comparison with explicit batching. The pinned Rust container reports
  nine passing tests and zero failures in 0.19 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 24, an unconditional preprocessing/query direct sum in the
  degree-explicit algebraic-selector model. On each normalized correspondence
  curve, a degree-`d` leaf morphism hits the `N`-point factor base at no more
  than `dN` witness points; summing over charged branch projection-degree mass
  `Delta` gives `E[Z] <= B N Delta / n`. Collecting
  `Theta(N)` rows forces `B Delta = Omega(n)` and hence `Omega(sqrt(n))` total
  work. Adaptive branch choice, multivalued root recovery, and direct
  non-materializing leaf output are included; compact shared DAGs without
  expanded-degree charging remain open.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded finite replay of the selector fibre-mass counting kernel.
  The pinned Rust container reports ten passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 25 to prevent an invalid extension of the degree-explicit
  model: multiplication-by-`k` has degree `k^2` but an `O(log k)`
  double-and-add DAG. Expanded degree is therefore not compact-circuit work.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 26, a degree-free direct sum for compact single-valued global
  maps. The rigidity lemma makes every `E -> E` map a translation of an
  endomorphism, whose restriction to the prime-order subgroup is constant or
  injective. Each explicit branch therefore covers at most `N` targets,
  giving `E[Z] <= B N r / n` and `Omega(sqrt(n))` total work for an
  `r`-branch catalogue. Succinct correspondences and implicitly exponential
  branching remain outside the theorem. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a finite cyclic replay of the constant-or-injective branch bound. The
  pinned Rust container reports eleven passing tests and zero failures in
  0.20 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 27 for the finite-domain boundary. The reduced coordinate
  algebra on the subgroup points is the full function algebra, so arbitrary
  relation selectors have dense `O(n)` lookup representations and their leaf
  functions need not be constant or injective. This table is not sub-rho, but
  it proves that global morphism rigidity cannot settle succinct finite-domain
  circuits; a genuine compression/query lower bound is required.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded cyclic example whose factor-base triples cover every target
  and whose selected leaf functions are neither constant nor injective. The
  pinned Rust container reports twelve passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 28 for symbolic recovery from succinct correspondences. A
  straight-line query that remains in the target function field and recovers
  a generic root defines a rational section; composing with its leaf
  projections yields the global rational maps already covered by Theorem 26.
  A remaining escape must use algebraic root extraction, finite-field
  branching, or finite-domain-only correctness. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 29 as an exact finite-field root-recovery counterexample.
  For primes `p = 3 mod 4`, the generic correspondence `y^2=t` has no section
  over `F_p(t)`, while `t^((p+1)/4)` selects a square root on every quadratic
  residue using `O(log p)` multiplications. This is not an elliptic relation
  algorithm, but it blocks any degree- or size-only extension of Theorem 28.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded native replay of the square-root selector. The pinned
  Rust container reports thirteen passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 30, showing that even per-leaf factor-base hit bounds fail
  for arbitrary finite-domain circuits. If `d` divides `p-1`, the compact map
  `t^((p-1)/d)` sends every target into the `d+1` element set consisting of
  zero and the `d`-th roots of unity; fixed branches can relabel that set by
  elliptic factor-base points. The map does not solve the joint decomposition
  constraint, which is therefore essential to any remaining barrier.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a native replay of the exact power-map image size. The pinned
  Rust container reports fourteen passing tests and zero failures in
  0.20 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 31 for the exact joint-entropy boundary. Any valid
  selector from targets to `m` factor-base leaves is injective because the sum
  of its output tuple recovers the target. Hence `|U| <= N^m`, with joint
  entropy `log |U|`; at least one leaf image has size `|U|^(1/m)`. This is
  tight for a set-theoretic choice of one tuple per nonempty sum fibre, so it
  reproduces tuple counting without a computational lower bound.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Extended the bounded finite-domain selector replay with the joint-image and
  product-of-leaf-images inequalities. The pinned Rust container still
  reports fourteen passing tests and zero failures, now in 0.19 seconds.
  `[pcc:unverified]`

  references resolved and no overfull boxes. New-paper prose lint reports zero
  findings; all mathematical assertions remain `[pcc:unverified]`.
- Added successor Theorem 18, an exact direct-sum bound for static-provenance
  product trees with arbitrary target-adaptive query branching. For `D`
  pre-target candidate leaves and `B` uniform targets it proves
  `E[Z] <= BD/n`; charging the explicit provenance and optimizing under
  `D <= N^m` gives the fixed-arity exponent `m/(2m-1)`. The proof does not use
  a random-map heuristic, but it deliberately excludes compact coordinate
  circuits that generate new target-dependent subtrees. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Extended the finite Rust checker with the multiplicity identity underlying
  Theorem 18 and updated the proof map, decision gates, and explicit boundary.
- Added Proposition 19, identifying balanced split-tree witness recovery with
  group `k`SUM-Indexing. Audited Dinur--Golovnev's ICALP 2026 trade-off in an
  attacker-favourable model: after substituting exact relation-output demand,
  its exponent is
  `1/2 + (3/4)/(2m - 1/2 - 2 delta) > 1/2` for `m >= 3`. This assumes, without
  claiming, free transport from integer to elliptic-group inputs and ignores
  extra preprocessing work, so restoring omitted costs cannot improve the
  exponent. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Recorded the primary data-structure sources and the lower-bound frontier in
  `LITERATURE.md`. The 2023/2026 papers confirm that strong adaptive
  3SUM-Indexing bounds beyond the logarithmic/tiny-probe regime remain a major
  open data-structure problem; this calibrates rather than proves the full
  ARQP conjecture. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 20, the exact exponent balance for an implicit one-witness
  data structure with preprocessing `N^s` and query cost `N^t`. The
  factor-base cap creates two regimes: if `s-t >= 1`, the optimized exponent
  is `s/(s+m-1-t)` and crosses rho exactly when `s+t < m-1`; if `s-t < 1`,
  the optimum approaches `alpha=1/m` and crosses rho exactly when
  `t < m/2-1`. Replacing `s` by `max(s,2)` incorporates the conventional
  `N^2` sparse linear-algebra charge. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Replayed `examples/finite_count_check.rs` in the isolated
  `rust:1.83-alpine` image pinned by digest
  `sha256:0ac946ed7597a9f053a1be2ce38c09aa88b3d7079a91ea491493615294b1f699`.
  All three tests passed, including direct grid minimization of Theorem 20's
  piecewise formula. This resolves the earlier missing-host-toolchain issue
  without claiming independent verification. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 20 by removing its one-witness restriction. The exact
  tuple-count bound applies to every verified decomposition returned across a
  batch, so `t` now denotes amortized target-dependent work after arbitrary
  multipoint sharing. Output and verification remain charged; the same
  piecewise exponent formula follows. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added exact `m=3,4,5` sanity rows for the audited 2026 indexing family over
  `delta in {0,1/2,1}`. The resulting fractions range from `11/19` to `5/7`;
  all remain strictly above one-half. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Extended and replayed the native checker for those nine exact fractions.
  The pinned Rust container now reports four passing tests, zero failures.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 21 for exact homomorphic bucket filters. A homomorphism out of
  the prime-order subgroup is trivial or injective; global algebraic-group
  maps from an elliptic curve to `G_a` or `G_m` are trivial; and an isogeny of
  degree below the subgroup order is injective on that subgroup. This closes
  exact additive/multiplicative quotient labels and low-degree isogeny fibres,
  but not non-homomorphic or one-sided-error filters. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Replayed the expanded native checker in the pinned Rust container: five
  tests passed and zero failed, including the small prime cyclic-group
  homomorphism kernel. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 21 from group-valued homomorphisms to arbitrary exact
  compositional labels `psi(P+Q)=psi(P) star psi(Q)`. Equality of labels is a
  group congruence whose fibres are cosets of a subgroup, even when `star` has
  no assumed algebraic laws; prime order again forces constant or injective
  labels. The remaining filters must therefore be non-compositional,
  approximate, or one-sided-error. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added an exhaustive finite replay over every smaller label map for cyclic
  prime orders 3, 5, and 7, accepting an arbitrary derived binary label table.
  The pinned Rust replay reports six passing tests and zero failures.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 21 again: any exact predicate of separate labels
  `R(lambda_A(P),lambda_B(Q),lambda_T(T))` for `P+Q=T` forces all three label
  maps to be injective, without assuming compositionality. The unresolved
  bucket filters must allow false positives, use target-specific functions,
  or avoid factoring through separate labels. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Exhaustively replayed the separate-label predicate obstruction for small
  cyclic groups. The pinned Rust run now has seven passing tests and zero
  failures. A `rustfmt` component check could not run because the pinned
  container's component download repeatedly failed; compilation itself is
  warning-free under the standalone test command. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 22 for one-sided separate-label filters. Collisions in either
  partial-sum label map inject into distinct false accepted triples, giving an
  exact label-fibre second-moment lower bound and, for uniform target batches,
  an expected false-verification lower bound. Joint encodings,
  target-dependent labels, and non-enumerating recovery remain outside the
  theorem. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded cyclic-group replay of the one-sided collision bound. The
  pinned Rust container reports eight passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Strengthened Theorem 22 to target-label collisions. Choosing one witness per
  attainable target injects ordered collisions inside each target-label fibre
  into distinct false triples, giving the same second-moment obstruction for
  all three separate label maps. The native fixture now replays all three
  bounds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 23 as a constructive target-dependent boundary. The label
  `x(T-P)` against a hash table keyed by `x(Q)` has only a sign false positive,
  but must scan a partial-sum side. Its best separate-query split has exponent
  `(m-1)/(2m-3)`, giving `2/3`, `3/5`, and `4/7` for arities 3, 4, and 5.
  This isolates search, rather than false verification, as the remaining
  bottleneck. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added an exact native arithmetic replay for the target-shift exponents and
  their comparison with explicit batching. The pinned Rust container reports
  nine passing tests and zero failures in 0.19 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 24, an unconditional preprocessing/query direct sum in the
  degree-explicit algebraic-selector model. On each normalized correspondence
  curve, a degree-`d` leaf morphism hits the `N`-point factor base at no more
  than `dN` witness points; summing over charged branch projection-degree mass
  `Delta` gives `E[Z] <= B N Delta / n`. Collecting
  `Theta(N)` rows forces `B Delta = Omega(n)` and hence `Omega(sqrt(n))` total
  work. Adaptive branch choice, multivalued root recovery, and direct
  non-materializing leaf output are included; compact shared DAGs without
  expanded-degree charging remain open.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded finite replay of the selector fibre-mass counting kernel.
  The pinned Rust container reports ten passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 25 to prevent an invalid extension of the degree-explicit
  model: multiplication-by-`k` has degree `k^2` but an `O(log k)`
  double-and-add DAG. Expanded degree is therefore not compact-circuit work.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 26, a degree-free direct sum for compact single-valued global
  maps. The rigidity lemma makes every `E -> E` map a translation of an
  endomorphism, whose restriction to the prime-order subgroup is constant or
  injective. Each explicit branch therefore covers at most `N` targets,
  giving `E[Z] <= B N r / n` and `Omega(sqrt(n))` total work for an
  `r`-branch catalogue. Succinct correspondences and implicitly exponential
  branching remain outside the theorem. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a finite cyclic replay of the constant-or-injective branch bound. The
  pinned Rust container reports eleven passing tests and zero failures in
  0.20 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 27 for the finite-domain boundary. The reduced coordinate
  algebra on the subgroup points is the full function algebra, so arbitrary
  relation selectors have dense `O(n)` lookup representations and their leaf
  functions need not be constant or injective. This table is not sub-rho, but
  it proves that global morphism rigidity cannot settle succinct finite-domain
  circuits; a genuine compression/query lower bound is required.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded cyclic example whose factor-base triples cover every target
  and whose selected leaf functions are neither constant nor injective. The
  pinned Rust container reports twelve passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Theorem 28 for symbolic recovery from succinct correspondences. A
  straight-line query that remains in the target function field and recovers
  a generic root defines a rational section; composing with its leaf
  projections yields the global rational maps already covered by Theorem 26.
  A remaining escape must use algebraic root extraction, finite-field
  branching, or finite-domain-only correctness. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 29 as an exact finite-field root-recovery counterexample.
  For primes `p = 3 mod 4`, the generic correspondence `y^2=t` has no section
  over `F_p(t)`, while `t^((p+1)/4)` selects a square root on every quadratic
  residue using `O(log p)` multiplications. This is not an elliptic relation
  algorithm, but it blocks any degree- or size-only extension of Theorem 28.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a bounded native replay of the square-root selector. The pinned
  Rust container reports thirteen passing tests and zero failures in
  0.18 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 30, showing that even per-leaf factor-base hit bounds fail
  for arbitrary finite-domain circuits. If `d` divides `p-1`, the compact map
  `t^((p-1)/d)` sends every target into the `d+1` element set consisting of
  zero and the `d`-th roots of unity; fixed branches can relabel that set by
  elliptic factor-base points. The map does not solve the joint decomposition
  constraint, which is therefore essential to any remaining barrier.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added a native replay of the exact power-map image size. The pinned
  Rust container reports fourteen passing tests and zero failures in
  0.20 seconds. `[pcc:unverified]`
- Added Proposition 31 for the exact joint-entropy boundary. Any valid
  selector from targets to `m` factor-base leaves is injective because the sum
  of its output tuple recovers the target. Hence `|U| <= N^m`, with joint
  entropy `log |U|`; at least one leaf image has size `|U|^(1/m)`. This is
  tight for a set-theoretic choice of one tuple per nonempty sum fibre, so it
  reproduces tuple counting without a computational lower bound.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
- Extended the bounded finite-domain selector replay with the joint-image and
  product-of-leaf-images inequalities. The pinned Rust container still
  reports fourteen passing tests and zero failures, now in 0.19 seconds.
  `[pcc:unverified]`
